# ApexCrew Agent Log

This chronological log records material agent work and human decisions. Future implementation entries must include the `PLAN.md` task, Superpowers skill, red/green evidence, commit or PR, and any manual correction. Never record credentials, private prompt text, or secret-bearing command output.

## 2026-08-04 / M1-FIX-004 - random Target Reservation identity

- **Skill**: `tdd`; the exact public store/control seams were recorded before adding the two required test nodes, and the failing selector preceded production changes.
- **Pre-task record**: fresh implementation session `/root/r3_03_fix004_impl` on clean base `1d0a9bd7f95a996ef3ecb20ab74297b3f8a14a90`. Allowed paths are `src/apexcrew/domain/admission.py`, `src/apexcrew/adapters/state/memory.py`, `src/apexcrew/adapters/state/sqlite.py`, `tests/contract/test_state_store.py`, `tests/integration/test_target_reservation.py`, and this log only. No commit, push, PR, or out-of-scope edit is authorized.
- **Public seams**: `InMemoryStateStore.create_bootstrap_run` and `SqliteStateStore.create_bootstrap_run` consume the control envelope; `SqliteStateStore` reopen plus identical command replay verifies durable receipt behavior. Tests may use public read queries to prove no additional Run/reservation/revision/Audit mutation.
- **Required behavior**: a store-owned injectable finite source supplies only `reservation-` plus 32 lowercase hexadecimal characters. Candidate selection and uniqueness occur inside the memory lock or one SQLite `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. Invalid values close with `TARGET_RESERVATION_ID_SOURCE_INVALID`; sixteen collisions or source exhaustion close with `TARGET_RESERVATION_ID_EXHAUSTED`, each as a one-time `CONFLICT` receipt with no Run/reservation/revision/Audit mutation and byte-identical replay. Production defaults are cryptographically random; no command payload selects an ID.
- **Red evidence**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/contract/test_state_store.py::test_target_reservation_id_source_retries_collisions_and_exhausts_closed tests/integration/test_target_reservation.py::test_target_reservation_id_persists_across_sqlite_restart_and_identical_replay -q` exited `1` with the two parameterized state-store cases and SQLite restart case failing. Each current constructor rejects `target_reservation_id_source`; therefore the existing digest-derived allocator cannot satisfy injected identity, collision, exhaustion, or replay contracts.
- **Implementation**: `admission.py` owns the strict `reservation-` plus 32 lowercase-hex validator, sixteen-attempt allocator, typed closed failure, and `secrets.token_hex(16)` production source. Both state stores inject a test source only at construction, select/check uniqueness while holding their existing exclusive lock or `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction, and never accept an ID through a command payload. Source-invalid, collision-exhausted, and source-exhausted creation attempts persist an independent bootstrap receipt with `CONFLICT`, no Run ID, and no sequence, so they add no Run, reservation, revision, or Audit row; a matching replay returns the stored outcome before consulting the source.
- **Green evidence**: the exact red selector reran after the final candidate and exited `0` with `3 passed`. The affected full test files `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/contract/test_state_store.py tests/integration/test_target_reservation.py -q` exited `0` with `80 passed`. `uv run --python 3.12 mypy src` exited `0` with `Success: no issues found in 33 source files`; scoped Ruff format/check exited `0` with `5 files already formatted` and `All checks passed!`; working-tree and cached `git diff --check` both exited `0` with no output.
- **Boundary**: only the six authorized paths are changed. No commit, push, PR, review, M1 ledger update, or later R3/M2-M4 work occurred in this session. Fresh ordered task spec-compliance and code-quality reviews remain required before the implementation commit.
- **Task spec-compliance review rejected**: the reviewer found that concurrent identical `CreateRun` calls could interleave the prior allocation rollback, bootstrap receipt insertion, and normal command-receipt insertion, leaving contradictory terminal outcomes; it also found that a non-string source result reached `len()` rather than closing as `TARGET_RESERVATION_ID_SOURCE_INVALID`. This review conclusion is superseded by the pending corrective red-green cycle. The corrective contract adds deterministic memory/two-connection SQLite races for failure-first and success-first source order, plus an explicit non-string source assertion.
- **Review-remediation red**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/contract/test_state_store.py::test_target_reservation_id_source_retries_collisions_and_exhausts_closed tests/contract/test_state_store.py::test_concurrent_create_run_arbitrates_bootstrap_receipts tests/integration/test_target_reservation.py::test_target_reservation_id_persists_across_sqlite_restart_and_identical_replay -q` exited `1`. Both backend variants raised `TypeError` for a numeric source result; the SQLite failure-first race returned distinct conflict/success outcomes; and the success-first race raised `STALE_SEQUENCE` in the competing creator.
- **Review-remediation implementation**: memory now routes CreateRun through one `RLock`-held receipt lookup and creation path, so source allocation, accepted command receipt, or bootstrap conflict receipt cannot interleave for one request ID. SQLite now uses one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` to inspect both receipt tables, allocate/check the candidate, then commit either the no-Run bootstrap conflict receipt or the Run/reservation/revisions/audit plus normal command receipt. The source boundary is typed as `object` and explicitly closes every non-string value as `TARGET_RESERVATION_ID_SOURCE_INVALID`.
- **Review-remediation green**: the corrective focused selector exited `0` with `7 passed`; it proves memory and two SQLite connections serialize failure-first and success-first identical CreateRun races to one byte-identical terminal/replay outcome with exactly one source read. The affected test files exited `0`; `uv run --python 3.12 mypy src` exited `0` with `Success: no issues found in 33 source files`; scoped Ruff format/check exited `0`; working-tree and cached `git diff --check` exited `0` with no output.
- **Review restart**: this code/test correction invalidates every previous task spec-compliance and code-quality conclusion. A fresh spec-compliance review and then a fresh code-quality review of the current staged candidate are required before any implementation commit.
- **Fresh spec-compliance review rejected**: the reviewer found that bootstrap failure receipts and normal control receipts still occupied disjoint global request-ID namespaces. A concurrent invalid-source CreateRun and a different normal control envelope could therefore each commit. The corrective contract introduces one atomic receipt claim for every control command in memory and SQLite, and races an invalid-source CreateRun against a different envelope on an existing Run in both first-winner orders. This review conclusion is superseded by the pending corrective red-green cycle.
- **Global-claim remediation red**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/contract/test_state_store.py::test_control_request_id_arbitrates_bootstrap_and_normal_commands -q` exited `1` with three failures. The partial memory migration still referenced the removed bootstrap receipt map, and SQLite let the invalid-source CreateRun and the ordinary `PausePayload` each produce their independent terminal outcome under one request ID.
- **Global-claim remediation implementation**: both stores now use one request-ID claim containing the canonical envelope digest and exact serialized outcome. Memory keeps this map inside its copy-and-publish transaction; SQLite migration 20 creates and backfills `control_request_claims`, with legacy normal receipts winning any historical duplicate. A claim is written before normal domain mutation, retained through accepted CreateRun and every unsequenced control outcome, and a losing different-envelope command returns `CONFLICT/IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSE` without a Run, reservation, revision, or Audit mutation. Legacy `command_receipts` remain available only for consumers such as Runtime Permit source validation.
- **Global-claim remediation green**: the new selector exited `0` with `4 passed`, proving memory and two independent SQLite connections arbitrate an invalid-source CreateRun against an ordinary command in both deterministic winner orders; a normal-first CreateRun does not consult its ID source. The reservation/exhaustion, identical CreateRun race, cross-path race, and restart selector exited `0` with `11 passed`; the two affected test files and full offline suite each exited `0`. `uv run --python 3.12 mypy src` exited `0` with `Success: no issues found in 33 source files`; scoped Ruff format/check and both working-tree and cached diff checks exited `0`.
- **Review restart**: this corrective code and test change supersedes every prior M1-FIX-004 review conclusion. A fresh spec-compliance review followed by a fresh code-quality review is required before any implementation commit.
- **Payload-binding remediation red**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/contract/test_state_store.py::test_request_id_reuse_with_result_never_validates_against_new_payload -q` exited `1` with both backends raising a payload-result validation error. The stores attempted to validate a stored `RevisionApprovalResult` against the new `PausePayload` before comparing full-envelope digests, rather than closing the request-ID reuse.
- **Payload-binding remediation implementation and green**: global-claim lookup now compares the stored and requested envelope digests before payload-specific outcome validation. An exact match replays the complete stored outcome; a different digest validates only the stored outcome identity with `result` excluded, then returns `CONFLICT/IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSE` with the stored Run and sequence. The focused selector exited `0` with `2 passed`; the two affected test files, full offline suite, mypy, and scoped Ruff format/check each exited `0`. This correction supersedes all earlier M1-FIX-004 review conclusions; a fresh spec-compliance review followed by code-quality review remains required before commit.
- **Fresh spec-compliance review rejected**: Start guard terminal paths (`PLAN_APPROVAL_BINDING_MISMATCH`, unavailable guard, guard denial, and guard binding mismatch) directly returned a `CommandOutcome` without a global claim, allowing an identical retry to reevaluate and mutate. The reviewer also found SQLite CreateRun claimed only after `_require_expected_sequence` had inserted `run_sequences` and after the other bootstrap rows. This conclusion is superseded by the corrective red-green cycle below.
- **Direct-outcome audit**: every direct terminal return reachable from `apply_control_command` or `create_bootstrap_run` was enumerated. CreateRun helper validation/allocation returns are persisted by its outer wrapper; accepted CreateRun claims before every bootstrap write. `RUN_NOT_FOUND`, `STALE_SEQUENCE`, and all four Start guard terminal outcomes are unsequenced control transactions and persist only the global claim, never an Audit row. All other reachable command outcomes use `_record_control_outcome`; `record_command` is a separate store test/persistence seam rather than a CrewControl dispatch path.
- **Start-guard remediation red**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/contract/test_state_store.py::test_start_guard_terminal_outcomes_are_globally_replayed -q` exited `1` with both state stores reaching `PRIVATE_REF_PRESTATE_MISMATCH` on replay. The new test proves that each first terminal result must block a later replay even when a valid guard is supplied.
- **Start-guard and CreateRun-order implementation**: every Start guard terminal branch now writes the same atomic unsequenced global claim in memory or SQLite. SQLite claims accepted CreateRun after allocation but before `run_sequences`, Run, reservation, input, revision, receipt, or Audit writes; its receipt insertion is deliberately non-claiming because the same transaction already owns the claim. SQL trace test `test_sqlite_create_run_claim_precedes_bootstrap_rows` asserts this strict order through every bootstrap table.
- **Start-guard and CreateRun-order green**: the Start guard replay selector exited `0` with `2 passed`; the SQL trace selector exited `0` with `1 passed`; the combined reservation, identical CreateRun, cross-path, payload-result, Start guard, trace, and restart selector exited `0` with `16 passed`. The two affected test files and full offline suite exited `0`; `uv run --python 3.12 mypy src` exited `0` with `Success: no issues found in 33 source files`; scoped Ruff format/check exited `0`. These source and test changes supersede every earlier M1-FIX-004 review conclusion. A new spec-compliance review followed by a new code-quality review is required before commit.

## 2026-07-25 / DISCOVERY-001

- **Skills**: `research`, `domain-modeling`.
- **Human context**: the user selected A-class Coding Agent Harness, named it ApexCrew, proposed repository-bounded action, HITL for dangerous operations, test-feedback correction, multi-agent cooperation, long context, and continuous cowork. Provider choice is deferred.
- **Agent work**: read both course requirement files; inspected the docs-only workspace and local toolchain; delegated primary-source GitHub landscape research and an independent scope review.
- **Finding**: generic multi-agent/worktree/persistence/evidence orchestration overlaps OpenHands, Vibe Kanban, Gas Town, h5i, Bernstein, Codex, and Ruflo. This invalidated novelty claims for individual durability features, not the user's authority to select the product direction.
- **Decision proposed, not accepted**: research suggested adversarial acceptance as an alternative mainline. It remained advisory.
- **Human intervention**: none during the research pass. User sign-off remained required before formal `SPEC.md` brainstorming.
- **Artifacts**: `docs/research/github-agent-landscape.md`, `INITIALIZATION.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, `docs/learning/README.md`, and revised `AGENTS.md`.
- **Lesson**: feature combinations that sounded distinctive were already implemented by direct competitors. Research must precede scope freeze, and provenance must not be confused with semantic correctness.

## 2026-07-26 / INIT-002 - Product direction accepted

- **Skills**: no Superpowers workflow was invoked; this was a human product decision informed by the completed research agents.
- **Key context**: choose one mainline, cap v1 at three Workers, use Python + TypeScript fixtures, and keep the assessed loop independent of host Coding Agent CLIs.
- **Human decision**: accepted **Evidence-Driven Durable Crew** as the only mainline; limited v1 to one repository and at most three Workers; selected Python + TypeScript fixtures and a public personal GitHub repository; prioritized backend and Agent-engineering interview value.
- **Core constraint**: real providers use low-level model APIs. Codex, Claude Code, Gemini CLI, and high-level agent frameworks cannot replace the self-built WorkerLoop.
- **Correction**: adversarial acceptance and weak-oracle challenge became supporting experiments only. The research report was marked advisory and its conflicting Codex-backend recommendation was removed.
- **Artifacts**: `docs/adr/0001-evidence-driven-durable-crew.md`, the decision callout in `docs/research/github-agent-landscape.md`, and reconciled initialization documents.
- **Lesson**: research may challenge novelty and refine claims, but it does not silently supersede an explicit human product decision.

## 2026-07-26 / SPEC-R2 - Mechanism and state approval

- **Skills**: Superpowers `brainstorming` with its accepted visual companion; `domain-modeling` for the approved vocabulary correction.
- **Key context**: turn the Round 1 freshness claim into a self-owned WorkerLoop, invalidation algorithm, evidence boundary, recoverable state model, and cross-language falsification fixtures without creating implementation.
- **Human decisions**: selected declared dependencies with conservative fallback; prepared-snapshot verification; one typed action per model turn; reconciliation-based recovery; private Run Branch promotion; action-boundary stale termination; and immutable Plan Revisions with bounded automatic correction. The user then explicitly approved the consolidated Round 2 design.
- **Independent review**: three parallel reviewers challenged integration identity, crash windows, state terminology, and fixture falsifiability. Their accepted corrections split Task Candidate from Run Candidate, separated immutable Evidence Receipts from Freshness Assessments, required a final run-wide gate, and bound integration to prepared commit and expected target OIDs.
- **Fixtures approved**: Python changes money units from cents to decimal dollars; TypeScript changes timestamps from milliseconds to seconds. Both preserve text-level mergeability while making old green evidence semantically invalid.
- **Rejected**: worker-tip receipts, merge-then-test, dynamic-only dependency inference, multi-action/free-form Worker execution, unconditional replay, hard process interruption, per-check human approval, and silent DAG/contract mutation.
- **Implementation state**: none. Round 3, consolidated `SPEC.md`, `writing-plans`, and independent cold-start review remain mandatory gates.
- **Commit**: `docs: approve brainstorming round 2`.
- **Lesson**: evidence is immutable history; current authority is a separate judgment over the exact prospective revision, dependency graph, contract, policy, and checks.

## 2026-07-26 / SPEC-R3 - Operations and acceptance approval

- **Skills**: Superpowers `brainstorming` and visual companion for consolidated architecture review; `openai-docs` for current model/API/pricing facts; `codebase-design` for deep-module seams; `domain-modeling` for canonical Round 3 language and qualifying ADRs.

The user reviewed the rendered Round 3 page rather than approving an unseen prose rewrite.

- **Schedule**: deadline supplied as 2026-08-10 and interpreted as 23:59 Asia/Shanghai; 25 hours/week yields approximately 53 hours. Optional weak-oracle, extra providers/platforms, writable WebUI, and hosted execution are cut before required core or course work.

The accepted workflow pins `open-design-v0.16.1` at commit `276b4d8e970bc143d7ad060181a89a834e3d9caf`, authors the custom ApexCrew Operational system through `design-md`, uses disposable `dashboard` and `design-review` artifacts, keeps the design contract original, and excludes Open Design packages/daemon/generated source from runtime and CI.

- **Conservative defaults approved**: token-protected loopback UI; Tier 1 retained until explicit purge and Tier 2 retained 30 days/1 GiB; regular-file actions/checks allowed, protected mutations revision-bound and one-use approved, escape/shell/network/socket/push/destructive Git hard-denied; evidence bound to an exact Execution Fingerprint and model actions to immutable provenance.
- **Agent output**: replaced every Round 3 gap in `SPEC.md` with normative operations, security, delivery, non-functional, schedule, risk, and acceptance clauses; reconciled process, glossary, architecture, initialization, README, contributor guidance, and ADRs.
- **Implementation state at Round 3 close**: none. `PLAN.md` was gated on architecture comparison, independent spec review, and final written-spec sign-off; source, fixtures, tests, and CI additionally required planning and cold-start review.
- **Human approval**: the user explicitly approved the complete Round 3 review page. This approves the design round and documentation update, not implementation or push.
- **Lesson**: durability is only credible when operational limits, threat assumptions, evidence environment, redaction, public-demo authority, and delivery time are as explicit and testable as the state machine.

## 2026-07-26 / SPEC-R3A - Independent Round 3 correction

- **Skills and context**: `code-review` ran independent Standards and Spec axes over `git diff --cached HEAD` from fixed point `fa780b7`; standards were `AGENTS.md`, both course requirement files, and `SPEC_PROCESS.md`, while the approved Round 3 record was the spec source.
- **Subagent evidence**: Standards reported a deadlock between retaining `PLAN.md` and using it for cold-start review; Spec reported that Tier 2 could exceed 1 GiB during an active Run and that the public replay lacked objective behavior.
- **Corrections**: made `PLAN.md` the allowed post-sign-off review input; added candid process reflection and technical-choice rationale; bounded every Tier 2 payload and repository total; specified replay controls and acceptance; restored the inclusive ten-minute CI bound and major-change design review; clarified bootstrap/no-progress pauses, credential injection risk, check timeouts, and sanitized disposable executor snapshots.
- **Human intervention**: none in this correction pass. It applies the already approved Round 3 choices and course rules; it does not add a product decision, implementation authority, or push approval.
- **Implementation state**: none. The correction changes documentation and review evidence only; no source, fixture, test, CI, or `PLAN.md` was created.
- **Lesson**: a high-level safety choice is incomplete until retention overflow, malicious check writes, secret-bearing untracked files, retry accounting, and user-visible replay behavior have explicit failure-closed outcomes.

## 2026-07-26 / SPEC-ARCH - A-Hybrid architecture approval

- **Skills and context**: `codebase-design` Design It Twice compared module interfaces after Round 3; `domain-modeling` kept implementation names out of the domain glossary and recorded the qualifying decision in ADR-0006. Two design agents ran in parallel; the journey-facade brief ran as a read-only follow-up after the agent-thread limit rejected a third concurrent spawn. Each pass used `SPEC.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, `INITIALIZATION.md`, and the system overview.
- **Subagent evidence**: the minimal kernel offered `execute/read` but warned of a swollen kernel and blocking interruption semantics; the flexible dual reactor improved locality but exposed many ordering-sensitive interfaces; the journey facade simplified CLI use but warned its continuation token could become a generic command bus.
- **Agent recommendation**: combine the minimal external surface with internal dual-reactor locality: `CrewControl.handle`, `CrewRuntime.run_until_blocked`, and `RunQueries.get`, backed by internal Coordinator, WorkerLoop, Admission, Authority, EffectJournal/recovery, tools, and projection modules.
- **Human decision**: the user explicitly approved **A-Hybrid**. This selects implementation module ownership and interface placement; it does not approve the complete written SPEC, create `PLAN.md`, authorize implementation, or authorize push.
- **Output**: updated the normative module contracts, architecture maps, initialization/process status, contributor guidance, README, learning-note index, and ADR-0006. `CONTEXT.md` remains unchanged because the new names are implementation interfaces rather than domain language.
- **Implementation state**: none. No package directory, fixture, test, CI, or `PLAN.md` was created.
- **Lesson**: the strongest shape is not the smallest interface alone; depth also requires local internal ownership so freshness/admission and recovery changes do not turn one kernel into a maintenance hotspot.

## 2026-07-26 / SPEC-ARCH-R1 - Whole-spec cold-review correction

- **Trigger**: after A-Hybrid approval, a fresh implementer/state-safety read of the complete SPEC found nine blocking ambiguities that the earlier architecture-diff review did not expose.
- **Skills and agents**: `codebase-design` preserved the approved deep-module seams; `domain-modeling` synchronized changed meanings in `CONTEXT.md`; read-only lifecycle/durability and concurrency/authority agents independently specified failure cases and deterministic guards.
- **Blocking evidence**: bootstrap planning had no executable owner/sequence; provider calls preceded durable intent; Plan approval bypassed Policy/Budget/Model Configuration start guards; future writers could stale already-promoted work; consumed Grants, old-head leases, unscoped reads, moved targets, and human uncertainty resolution had contradictory or open semantics.
- **Correction**: added a bounded Coordinator planning protocol, explicit `PLANNING`/`READY_TO_START` gates, durable model request reservations, promotion-hazard validation, scoped `R`/`D`/`W`/`Q` access, classified-head lease admissibility, pinned-base behavior, exact-intent Grant settlement, and closed objective `INDETERMINATE` choices. Candidate CAS remains Admission-owned; bootstrap CLI flows have no Run/model/repository effect authority.
- **Implementation state**: none. The changes are documentation only; `PLAN.md`, source, fixtures, tests, and CI remain prohibited.
- **Review state**: correction prepared and awaiting a new whole-SPEC cold read. A-Hybrid approval remains valid, but this entry is not final written-spec approval or push authorization.
- **Lesson**: interface shape can pass a diff review while lifecycle ordering remains impossible; every external effect and pre-activation phase needs the same durable authority and failure semantics as the main Worker loop.

## 2026-07-26 / SPEC-ARCH-R2 - Authority and host-safety closure

## 2026-07-31 / COURSE-GAP-1 - Graded course requirements not carried by the current plan

A re-read of the course brief against the repository found requirements that `SPEC.md` and the R3 `PLAN.md` do not fully carry. These are graded deliverables, not preferences.

- **PR workflow is mandatory and is currently unmet.** Brief 4.6 requires one worktree per independent feature/large module, each corresponding to one PR; 4.7 rejects a single commit containing everything and requires a complete commit and PR history. All 12 commits to date went directly to `main` with zero PRs, and `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md` has never been used. Documentation-only commits are defensible, but M1 implementation must move to worktree-plus-PR boundaries.
- **Attribution format**: 4.7 requires commit or PR text to state which subagent performed the work and which parts a human changed. Existing commits carry `Co-Authored-By` but not that split.
- **Two-phase per-task review**: 4.6.4 requires a spec-compliance check followed by a code-quality check after every task, with critical issues fixed before the next task. The current sizing does not visibly include this cost.
- **Continuous `PLAN.md` updates**: 4.7 requires marking each task complete with its commit hash as it lands.
- **Submission channel**: deliverable list 5 submits through one NJU GitLab repository link; GitHub is development only. No NJU remote is configured - `origin` is the sole remote. Deliverable 6 requires `.gitlab-ci.yml` with a job named exactly `unit-test`, and deliverable 7 requires the **last CI run to be green**, which is execution work rather than a file drop.
- **Deliverable 9 tension with the signed scope**: the course requires a publicly reachable URL exposing an accessible WebUI. `SPEC.md` v0.1 excludes hosted backend, production public execution, and a writable WebUI, and its only public surface is a sanitized static fixture replay on GitHub Pages. Whether that satisfies deliverable 9 is a grader judgment. It must be decided explicitly and described accurately in `README.md` rather than left implicit.
- **`REFLECTION.md` authorship**: brief section 6 requires the student to write it and forbids AI ghost-writing, permitting disclosed AI polish. No agent may draft its content. Planning its schedule and required questions is permitted.
- **`SECURITY.md`** is named in the `INITIALIZATION.md` documentation table but does not exist; either create it or correct the table.

These findings are inputs to the M1 `PLAN.md` revision, not authorization to change `SPEC.md`. Where a requirement conflicts with the signed specification, the resolution is an explicit owner decision, recorded, and if necessary a proposed specification amendment with a new digest.

## 2026-07-31 / DELIVERY-1 - Delivery channel resolved to GitHub only

- **Owner decision**: no NJU/GitLab remote will be configured; GitHub is the sole remote and delivery channel.
- **Documented basis and its limits**: the course brief is internally inconsistent. Section 4.7 is titled "GitHub 仓库要求", mandates a public GitHub repository, and section 4.8's CI clause requires GitHub Actions on every push; section 5 instead describes submission "通过同一个 NJU Git 仓库链接" and names `.gitlab-ci.yml` as deliverable 6. The recorder searched both course documents exhaustively and found no clause resolving the conflict. The owner resolved it in favour of GitHub using course context not present in these files. This entry records the decision and its basis; it does not claim the brief itself authorizes dropping the NJU link.
- **Retained despite the decision**: `.gitlab-ci.yml` carrying a job named exactly `unit-test` is still produced. Deliverable 6 names the file directly, and frozen `SPEC.md` section 10.5 requires the file's existence and job name rather than a GitLab remote, so keeping it satisfies both without a specification amendment.
- **Residual risk, not closed**: if the grader requires the NJU link as the submission channel, GitHub-only delivery does not satisfy deliverable 5. Configuring a second remote later is a single command, so the cost of reversing this decision stays low; the cost of discovering it late does not.
- **Documents corrected**: `README.md`, `INITIALIZATION.md`, and `AGENTS.md` now state GitHub-only delivery, the passed Stage 4 review, the M1 plan revision as the next gate, and the 2026-07-31 host re-verification. `AGENTS.md` additionally carries the course workflow rules that bind from M1: worktree-per-module with one PR each, subagent/human attribution in commit or PR text, the two-phase per-task review, and continuous `PLAN.md` commit-hash updates.
- **`SPEC.md`**: unchanged and unaffected. Its `.gitlab-ci.yml` requirement remains satisfiable.

Only its seven table resource categories, represented by eight scalar ceilings, plus the dated returned-model price mapping are Budget proposal fields. Per-Task tranche/call/Attempt/stale/resume/no-progress limits, action/check timeouts, provider retries, and the warning percentage are fixed code-owned `V01_MECHANISM_LIMITS`; they are rejected as Budget extras and not accepted from caller-controlled model requests. ## 2026-07-31 / PLAN-M1-REVIEW - Independent M1 document review returns 10 blockers

- **Subject**: M1-R1 candidate `PLAN.md` at SHA-256 `C19C351A877351214C9D915A6EE23A79AA9FC9EE6C52ADAA879B91F03B6EE5AD`, repository at `60a7331`. Reviewer confirmed that digest, the frozen `SPEC.md` digest, and the M0 archive digest before starting.
- **Verdict**: `10 BLOCKERS`. **M1 stops before Task 1.** Module coverage passed cleanly: Tasks 1-17 map to 27 slices across 8 module PRs with no omission, duplication, or slice falling between modules.
- **Blocker 1, the consequential one**: the Budget narrow-reading decision cannot claim it needs no `SPEC.md` amendment. The recorder verified this independently and confirms the reasoning. `SPEC.md` line 481 states the table values "are also non-raiseable v0.1 administrative maxima", so a value can be a Budget Revision field **and** non-raiseable at the same time. Line 499 calls the per-Task limits "**likewise** non-raiseable" - pointing at that same dual status. The M1 plan's implicit premise, that non-raiseable implies not-a-Budget-field, is therefore defeated by line 481 itself. Line 493's enumeration of what a Budget Revision "may lower" still gives the narrow reading real support, and section 7 line 397's "allocation rules" plus "governs Run and Task allocation" gives the broad reading real support. Both readings are defensible, so the ambiguity is genuine and only an approved specification clarification can close it.
- **Blockers requiring `SPEC.md` text**: 1 (Budget field boundary), 2 (whether a table ceiling may be lowered to zero; the plan decided `ge=1` on its own), 3 (active Run time semantics - which lifecycle states bill, how approval waits and crash intervals count, start/stop transactions and restart rules; the plan has only a DTO and synthetic threshold tests, no trusted clock, persistent accumulation, or production check).
- **Blockers requiring plan correction only**: 4 (Task 9B calls undefined `_ceiling_for`, so its claimed red is unreachable, and seven of eight global usage metrics have no production caller), 5 (Task 9D calls three undefined functions and orders exhausted-lower-ceiling ahead of the approved-higher-Budget resume path that line 499 requires), 6 (Task 9C in M1-05 runs `tests/contract/test_executor.py`, created by Task 15 in M1-08, under strictly serial modules), 7 (Task 11B returns `GrantedActionIntent`, defined by Task 17), 8 (three stale post-split selector paths), 9 (7B Step 14 writes `test_no_follow_handles.py` while 7B's exact stage set omits it), 10 (GitHub Actions deferred to M3 Task 35A although `SPEC.md` line 561 and course section 4.8 require CI on every push, and M1 performs eight module pushes first).
- **Independent verification by the recorder**: blockers 1, 6, 9, and 10 were checked directly against the cited lines and all four are real. Blocker 6 and 9 are literal execution failures, not stylistic notes. Sampling stopped there; the report's rigor is established.
- **Non-blocking findings**: six directory-level `git add` pathspecs that would absorb unintended changes, an imprecise 7A red condition, and a commit-count statement that was true when written and is now stale.
- **Process observation, recorded rather than smoothed**: `SPEC.md` was signed after three independent reviews each reporting zero blockers, yet a fourth reader working from the plan found three genuine specification gaps. This is the review process working, not failing, but it means the frozen specification was complete enough to sign and not complete enough to implement from. Future gates should treat implementation-facing review as capable of reopening specification text.
- **Implementation state**: none. No worktree, branch, or source file was created. `M1 GO` was not given and cannot be given until a revised plan passes a new independent review.

## 2026-07-31 / SPEC-AMEND-1 - Specification revision 2 approved and applied

- **Human decision**: the owner reviewed `docs/proposals/0001-spec-clarification-budget-and-run-time.md` at SHA-256 `6AA24E1717835BAC2D1325E0CB140E5FFC2705F5A2912C124223799E298120C6` and approved all three gaps as written. Approval was given against the exact proposed wording, not against a summary or an unwritten intent.
- **Superseded artifact**: `SPEC.md` revision 1, SHA-256 `2F1434AB29C3B7205B13CA96FE35D18C7666729F633EDF984F1DFCA54F0663BC`, signed 2026-07-27. It remains the authoritative identity for every decision recorded before this entry. Its exact pre-amendment bytes were copied outside the repository before any edit.
- **New artifact**: `SPEC.md` revision 2, SHA-256 `97E9652D874B606C1673867923C97C29834F63B43ADB3F3E89779B13183E26D6`, 131,011 bytes, 636 lines.
- **Applied diff**: 12 insertions, 2 deletions, confined to sections 7 and 10.2. The recorder printed the complete diff and confirmed it matched the approved wording verbatim before computing the new digest. Nothing outside the proposed text was altered.
- **Substance**: the Budget Revision field boundary is closed in favour of the narrow reading; scalar table ceilings other than the cost reserve are positive integers; active Run time accumulates only while runtime ownership is held, measured monotonically, with crash intervals closed conservatively at the last Audit Event of that ownership generation.
- **Deliberately not updated**: the rejected M1-R1 `PLAN.md` still cites revision 1 at its lines 15 and 40. It was left untouched so its reviewed identity `C19C351A...E5AD` stays valid, and its own `Frozen input` gate now fails against revision 2, structurally preventing execution of a rejected plan. M1-R2 must cite revision 2.
- **Historical records unchanged**: earlier `AGENT_LOG.md` and `SPEC_PROCESS.md` entries continue to cite revision 1 because they record what was true when written. Only current-state references were updated, namely `README.md`.
- **Authorization boundary**: this approval authorizes applying the text and recording the digest. It does not authorize implementation, `M1 GO`, credentials, push, or publication. Review blockers 4-10 remain open and belong to the M1-R2 plan revision.
- **New work this creates**: gap 3 requires a monotonic clock port, a durable cumulative total, interval open/close inside the existing permit-consumption and `RunStop` transactions, recovery closure from the last Audit Event, and an action-boundary check. Estimated 1.5-3 hours; M1-R1 had none of it.
- **Lesson**: a digest freezes bytes, not completeness. Three specification-only reviews agreed the text had no blockers; the first reader who tried to build from it found three. Freezing should be treated as a checkpoint that survives amendment, not as a claim that amendment will never be needed.

- **Revision-2 Gap 3 absorbed**: Tasks 9/9A add the durable cumulative/open-interval schema, state values, synthetic threshold red tests, and trusted active-time budget evaluation. Slices 11A/11B add the monotonic clock port/adapter, open the interval in the Permit-consumption transaction, close it in the `RunStop` transaction, seal orphaned generations at their last committed Audit Event, and check the persisted-plus-open duration at every action boundary.
- **First correction round, stated accurately**: the first verification correction fixed exactly the four Task 11 helpers supplied by that defect report, then reported a clean systemic private-helper sweep. That sweep claim was false: the check as performed did not cover the class. The Task 9D canonical-order and Task 17 Granted-Action recovery corrections remain genuine and unchanged.
- **Second private-helper verification round**: an exact CommonMark-fence sweep found 44 unresolved private calls: 11 already attributed in prose and 33 with neither definition nor mention. `_require_expected_sequence` was undefined on the first line of the same `_require_consumable_runtime_permit` function repaired in round 1; the original defect report itself named only four of that function's five missing helpers. Round 2 defines the safety-load-bearing subset in each owning task, removes Task 11's premature Task 24D unresolved-set call, and adds an exhaustive convention for the remaining prose-owned or safety-neutral factoring. The final sweep is 121 distinct definitions, 96 distinct calls, 13 unresolved, 13 prose-attributed, and zero silently undefined.
- **Zero-blocker pre-review polish**: after a zero-blocker verification pass performed by the assisting agent, this polish expanded line 30 from compressed ranges to the ledger's exact 27-slice order and documented why `ACTIVE_RUN_SECONDS` remains decimal despite its integer ceiling; that pass is not the independent review required by the Execution Gate because the assisting agent co-authored the correction rounds. The gate is unchanged: an owner-dispatched independent document review must still return zero blockers before the owner may write `M1 GO`.
- **Dispatch-closure repair basis**: what first appeared to be an owner design choice was resolved by rereading frozen SPEC revision-2 lines 299, 300, 302, 459, and 460. Those lines require dispatch closure before `PAUSED`, persistence through `INDETERMINATE`, closure after the current atomic action settles, and exact human resolution before reopening. Task 9 therefore owns a durable general Run flag plus canonical cause set; Task 9B closes it in the settlement transaction, Task 9D is the only Budget reopen CAS after approved higher capacity plus exact resume, and Task 11's barrier reason is only a derived copy. This is a specification-derived repair, not owner approval; the owner may reverse it if their reading of those frozen lines differs.
- **Capacity posture**: the revision/proposal/re-review round consumes an estimated 6-10 hours and leaves about 25 implementation hours. The revised M1 estimate remains 29.7-67.7 hours; M1 is a priority with explicit schedule risk, M2-M3 are unauthorized roadmap without a credible deadline claim, and M4 is post-M3 roadmap rather than a sprint promise.
- **Independent-review cross-check**: an owner-dispatched independent review by Gemini 3.1 Pro on 2026-08-01 returned four claimed blockers; three were not sustained on cross-check, because two arose from imprecise wording in the review prompt rather than plan defects and one is contradicted by the writers at `PLAN.md` lines 10947 and 11051, which already canonicalize before the readers validate; the fourth was genuine but was a test-diagnostics defect rather than a blocker and was fixed here together with two further instances the review did not find. The review verdict is not a pass and the gate is unchanged: a further owner-dispatched independent review must return zero blockers before the owner may write `M1 GO`.

An owner-dispatched independent M1-R2 document review must return zero blockers, after which only the owner may record `M1 GO` and separately authorize creation of the first worktree. Source Steps 1-2 and 8-20, including Step 14's later edit to `test_no_follow_handles.py`, remain untouched for Task 7B.
- **Implementing agent and skills**: OpenAI Codex (GPT-5), primary agent `/root`, used the `tdd`, `writing-plans`, and `andrej-karpathy-skills:karpathy-guidelines` skills. The existing approved `PLAN.md` was used instead of creating an additional planning document. No human edited code. Spec reviewer: `/root/task7a_spec_review`. Code-quality reviewer: `/root/task7a_quality_review`.
- **First TDD red evidence**: after writing `tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_no_follow_handles.py` and before creating any repository-adapter source, `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_no_follow_handles.py::test_every_component_is_opened_relative_to_its_held_parent -q` exited 1 during collection with exact text `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'apexcrew.adapters.repository'` and `ERROR: found no collectors`. The plan required that missing-module text but predicted collection exit code 4; the actual pytest/interpreter combination returned 1, which is recorded rather than rewritten.
- **Intermediate TDD evidence**: the first full slice run exited 1 with two test-support failures: ctypes function objects were unhashable in the signature fixture, and the duplicate-directory fixture omitted `NextEntryOffset`, so its second record was unreachable. Correcting only those fixtures made all then-present 18 cases pass. The spec review then proved that a backslash-containing Windows name let `NtCreateFile` resolve unchecked intermediate components; `test_backslash_cannot_hide_unchecked_windows_components` was added and exited 1 with all three `open`/`try_open`/`try_open_any` parameters failing to raise. After the three sibling guards rejected backslash with `INVALID_HANDLE_RELATIVE_PATH`, the selector exited 0 with three passes. The quality review then proved Python code-point length truncated an astral UTF-16 component before `NtCreateFile`; `test_windows_unicode_string_lengths_use_utf16_bytes` exited 1 with actual `[(4, 6, '<astral>')]` versus expected `[(6, 8, '<astral>x')]`. Explicit UTF-16LE storage and byte lengths made that selector exit 0.
- **Focused and final green evidence**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_no_follow_handles.py --collect-only -q` exited 0 with `23` tests. `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_no_follow_handles.py -q` exited 0 with all cases passing. `uv run --python 3.12 pytest -q` exited 0. `uv run --python 3.12 ruff format --check .` exited 0 with `38 files already formatted`; `uv run --python 3.12 ruff check .` exited 0 with `All checks passed!`; `uv run --python 3.12 mypy src` exited 0 with `Success: no issues found in 22 source files`. The exact-scope `git diff --check` and forbidden pathname-API scan exited 0.
- **Finding 1 - confirmed containment repair**: `StableHandleTree.try_open()` now copies `open()`'s exact `{"", ".", ".."}` rejection using `RepositoryUnsafeError("INVALID_HANDLE_RELATIVE_PATH")` and its cached-node kind rejection using `RepositoryUnsafeError("GIT_STORAGE_KIND_CHANGED")`. Tests cover `try_open("..")`, `try_open("")`, and cached kind change. The `try_open()` containment gap was found by attempting to implement the plan, not by any review; two independent document reviews and four correction rounds did not catch it. The owner-confirmed interpretation derives from SPEC line 519 and the two sibling methods, and the owner may reverse it if those sources are read differently.
- **Finding 2 - specified Windows helpers**: `_query_directory_handle_names` calls only `NtQueryDirectoryFile(FileIdBothDirectoryInformation)` on the supplied held directory handle, filters dot entries, and fails closed on malformed UTF-16, invalid offsets/lengths, duplicate names, or the requested maximum. `_last_status_is_object_name_not_found` accepts only `STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_NOT_FOUND` and `STATUS_OBJECT_PATH_NOT_FOUND`; `_last_status_is_not_a_directory` accepts only `STATUS_NOT_A_DIRECTORY`. Stale NT status is cleared before every new `NtCreateFile`, so a later reparse/identity failure cannot inherit file-fallback authority. No `Path.open`, `Path.exists`, `Path.iterdir`, `Path.resolve`, `lstat`, or pathname reopen/enumeration API was added.
- **Finding 3 - fully typed native wrappers**: every wrapped Win32/NT call has explicit `argtypes` and `restype`: `CreateFileW`, `GetFileInformationByHandle`, `SetFilePointerEx`, `ReadFile`, `CloseHandle`, `NtCreateFile`, and `NtQueryDirectoryFile`. `HANDLE` uses `wintypes.HANDLE`, a pointer-sized type, including `CreateFileW.restype`. `UNICODE_STRING` now uses explicit UTF-16LE bytes, preserves astral components, and rejects byte lengths that would overflow its `USHORT` fields.
- **Finding 4 - Step 6 test support**: `recording_backend`, `recording_windows_backend`, `recording_posix_backend`, `FILE_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT`, and `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` are defined inside the one authorized Task 7A test path. No support file outside the exact stage was required.
- **Finding 5 - coverage classification**: the retained parent-handle traversal and flag tests use simulated POSIX and Windows recording backends. The Windows-host tests load the real DLLs to inspect ctypes declarations but use a fake NT call for directory-buffer/status behavior; this is not host-native filesystem traversal coverage. The spec reviewer also ran a one-off real Windows traversal diagnostic to reproduce the hidden-backslash defect, but it is not a retained test. A real host-native Windows integration test is cheap and possible; it was not added unasked.
- **Ordered reviews**: `/root/task7a_spec_review` initially failed the diff for unchecked backslash traversal, then returned PASS after the red/green repair and found no remaining critical spec issue. Only afterward, `/root/task7a_quality_review` initially failed the diff for UTF-16 code-point/byte truncation, then returned PASS after the red/green repair and found no remaining critical correctness, ABI, status-lifecycle, cleanup, collection, or scope issue.
- **Changed paths**: `src/apexcrew/adapters/repository/__init__.py`, `src/apexcrew/adapters/repository/no_follow.py`, `src/apexcrew/adapters/repository/no_follow_posix.py`, `src/apexcrew/adapters/repository/no_follow_windows.py`, `tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_no_follow_handles.py`, and `AGENT_LOG.md`.
- **Commit subject**: `feat(git): add no-follow path handles`.
- **External state**: no push, publication, credential access, pathname-based repository enumeration, or Task 7B work was performed.

The plan implements `SecretPathPolicy.inspect` and `ActionPolicy.classify` as separate primitives: `ActionPolicy.default()` has no secret-policy dependency and, by itself, does not classify an otherwise canonical secret path as `DENY`; composition into effect enforcement is not specified in this slice. The prescribed digest test proves only that different rules change the opaque value, not its stated no-raw-rule projection. The mixed-case denial test proves generic returned fields but does not independently compare timing or inspect any Audit, WebUI, or log projection; none of those integration surfaces exists in Task 6. Step 17 asks for an “exact entry” that is explicitly labeled as an example and omits the prompt-required base SHA, exact commands/output, changed paths, and commit subject, so this evidence-complete entry supersedes that abbreviated example.

Migration 5 is additive from Task 4B's migration 4.
- **Locked-worktree inventory defect**: the plan modelled the locked worktree administration directory as three or four entries, while a real checked-out locked worktree from supported Git `2.47.1.windows.1` contains eight: `HEAD`, `commondir`, `gitdir`, `index`, `locked`, `logs`, `refs`, and `ORIG_HEAD`. Removing that overflow exposed and the same regression caught Git for Windows' `/` spelling for both absolute back-references; exact byte comparison now derives those two trusted expected paths with `Path.as_posix()` rather than accepting alternate paths.

Rerunning both selectors exited 0 with three parameter cases, and the complete reservation plus state-store contract selection exited 0 with 53 cases.

Human changes to retained files: none.

The tamper check therefore proves the exact top-level admin-name set and the exact required record/lock bindings, but does not prove anything about entries beneath those two directories; deep inspection is explicitly outside Task 8.
- **Typed Git and containment boundary**: production reservation code uses only closed `GitWorktreeAddNoCheckout` and `GitWorktreeLock` operations plus Task 7 no-follow handle traversal. No `Path.open`, `Path.exists`, `Path.iterdir`, `Path.resolve`, `lstat`, pathname reopen, raw shell, Win32/NT wrapper change, or modification to `try_open()` traversal/cached-kind guards was added.

## 2026-08-02 / M1-04 - Required Ubuntu CI portability repair

- **Base and scope**: this repair started from `aa15d1915c6b7b633568cd64c4d2f6730cfaf4f4` on `codex/m1-04-git-safety` and is attributable to `TASK-007A` and `TASK-007B`. OpenAI Codex (GPT-5), primary session `/root`, used the `diagnosing-bugs` and `andrej-karpathy-skills:karpathy-guidelines` skills. No subagent or human edited code.
- **Observed CI failures**: GitHub Actions `quality` failed on Ubuntu because Linux mypy treated the runtime `WinDLL = None` suppression as unused and could not infer `_kernel32` or `_ntdll`; `unit-ubuntu` failed four replacement-race parameter cases because `Path("C:/git.exe")` is relative on POSIX, plus two Windows simulations because Linux's four-byte `wchar_t` tried to interpret explicit UTF-16LE storage as native wide characters. The production `ABSOLUTE_GIT_EXECUTABLE_REQUIRED` denial was not relaxed, and no test or assertion was skipped or removed.
- **Typing red and focused green evidence**: before the repair, `uv run mypy --platform linux src/apexcrew/adapters/repository/no_follow_windows.py` exited 1 with 24 errors matching CI: one unused `type: ignore` and 23 unresolved `_kernel32`/`_ntdll` uses. After the repair, that exact command and its `--platform win32` counterpart each exited 0 with `Success: no issues found in 1 source file`; `uv run --python 3.12 mypy --platform linux src` exited 0 with `Success: no issues found in 24 source files`.
- **Runtime regression evidence**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_no_follow_handles.py::test_replaced_bound_component_stops_before_git_spawn tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_no_follow_handles.py::test_windows_unicode_string_lengths_use_utf16_bytes tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_no_follow_handles.py::test_windows_stale_not_directory_status_cannot_enable_file_fallback -q` exited 0 with seven parameter cases at `[100%]`. The fixture now resolves its executable path on the host, and the Windows fake reads the stored pointer as raw memory before strict UTF-16LE decoding, independent of host `wchar_t` width.
- **Final Windows mechanical gates**: `uv run --python 3.12 ruff format --check .` exited 0 with `43 files already formatted`; `uv run --python 3.12 ruff check .` exited 0 with `All checks passed!`; `uv run --python 3.12 pytest -q` exited 0 at `[100%]` with the two existing platform skips; `uv run --python 3.12 mypy src` exited 0 with `Success: no issues found in 24 source files`. Ubuntu is pending real CI.
- **Platform evidence limitation and lesson**: the Linux runtime failures could not be reproduced locally because this host is Windows and uses Windows' two-byte `wchar_t`; only the Linux mypy target could be reproduced locally. This is the second time M1-04 has shown that green local Windows gates are not evidence of a green required Windows-and-Ubuntu build. Cross-platform fixtures must use host-neutral paths and explicit wire encodings, and required-platform CI remains the authoritative portability evidence.
- **Preserved boundaries**: `StableHandleTree.try_open()` traversal and cached-kind guards, all native `argtypes`/`restype` declarations, the reservation inventory repair, debt #1 closure, and the absolute Git executable guard are unchanged. No push was performed.
- **Changed paths**: `src/apexcrew/adapters/repository/no_follow_windows.py`, `tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_no_follow_handles.py`, and `AGENT_LOG.md`.
- **Commit subject**: `fix(git): make Windows no-follow checks portable`.
- **Review boundary**: spec-compliance and code-quality reviews are deferred to the M1-04 module PR review.

## 2026-08-02 / TASK-009A - Stop repeated Task behavior

- **Base and task**: Task 9A (`TASK-009A`) started from `a24c10b009d5b9e773cc5be565246014173b3f22` on `codex/m1-05-authority`. OpenAI Codex (GPT-5), primary session `/root`, used the `tdd` and `andrej-karpathy-skills:karpathy-guidelines` skills. No subagent, assisting agent, or human edited the retained Task 9A files.
- **Actual first red**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_task_stopping.py::test_second_identical_checkpoint_pauses_task_and_closes_dispatch -q` exited 1 during collection with `ImportError: cannot import name 'CheckpointKey' from 'apexcrew.domain.authority'` and no collector. The plan's predicted missing-method `AttributeError` was unreachable because `CheckpointKey` and `TaskStopDecision` were imported before the test body. After those types and the Authority route existed, the selector next failed at the real storage gap with `AttributeError: 'SqliteStateStore' object has no attribute 'install_running_attempt_for_test'`, then at the absent `record_task_checkpoint` method.
- **Lifecycle-storage red/green**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/contract/test_state_store.py::test_task_and_attempt_lifecycle_storage_matches -q` first exited 1 for both adapters because `install_running_attempt_for_test` was absent, then exited 0 with both cases green. Task 9A creates storage the plan never allocated: additive migration 7 introduces authoritative `tasks` and `attempts`, plus required `task_checkpoints` and `task_invalid_actions` histories. The in-memory adapter owns matching records. The retained state vocabulary is deliberately minimal: Task `ACTIVE`, `READY`, `PAUSED` and Attempt `RUNNING`, `FAILED`; later slices must extend it additively.
- **Stop red/green evidence**: the checkpoint selector next exited 1 with missing `record_task_checkpoint`, then exited 0 at the fixed second identical observation. The invalid-action selector exited 1 with missing `record_invalid_action`, then exited 0 with three distinct, correctly Task-bound Attempt IDs becoming `FAILED` and Task successors `READY`, `READY`, `PAUSED`. SQLite restart, memory/SQLite parity, post-pause rejection, terminal-Attempt replay rejection, lease revocation, and injected state/Audit rollback selectors all pass.
- **Mechanism-limit authority**: both stop adapters read `V01_MECHANISM_LIMITS.repeated_checkpoint_ceiling` and `V01_MECHANISM_LIMITS.repeated_invalid_action_ceiling` directly. Their public signatures have no `ceiling` parameter or default. `test_task_stop_cap_is_not_caller_selectable` proves both SQLite and memory reject a caller-supplied cap for both methods before lifecycle, history, or Audit mutation.
- **Legal transitions**: `_set_task_state` compare-and-sets only the slice-required SPEC transitions `ACTIVE -> READY`, `ACTIVE -> PAUSED`, and the Task 9D-required `PAUSED -> READY`; `_finish_attempt` accepts only `RUNNING -> FAILED`. Invalid Task/Attempt source states raise `StateConflict` and transaction rollback preserves Task, Attempt, lease, history count, and Audit sequence. An `ACTIVE` Task now denies a second Attempt as `TASK_NOT_READY`, while a Run-wide dispatch closure retains reason precedence.
- **Runtime-boundary evidence**: the first exact active-runtime selector exited 1 with missing `AuthorityService.evaluate_active_run_time_boundary`, then with the missing SQLite method. Its green result proves ten observed owned seconds close dispatch, preserve the four-second cumulative value and open interval, and deny another Attempt as `ACTIVE_RUN_TIME_CEILING`. Memory and SQLite decisions match. A below-ceiling check returns `CONTINUE` without appending Audit. The adapter reads the monotonic clock inside the write boundary and uses that same reading for the stop event.
- **Protocol ownership**: stop and runtime-boundary methods were added to `AuthorityState`, not `EffectJournal`, because they compare-and-set authoritative lifecycle, budget, lease, dispatch, and counter state together with Audit. `AuthorityService` was already typed against `AuthorityState`; `EffectJournal` remains the narrower generic effect-intent journal. No `cast` or `Any` was added.
- **Counter semantics**: checkpoint and invalid-action histories are Task-lifetime records keyed independently by exact checkpoint/action identity. Alternating action kinds or identities does not reset either history, so a Worker cannot create an unbounded loop by alternating them.
- **Ordered reviews**: `/root` spec-compliance check returned `PASS` against SPEC sections 6.2 and 10.2 after confirming fixed adapter-owned caps, exact legal transitions, durable restart behavior, atomic invalid-action settlement, and trusted runtime timing. `/root` code-quality check first found the `ACTIVE`-Task scheduling edge and noncanonical SQLite dispatch-cause acceptance; focused regression/validation fixes were applied. The final code-quality check returned `PASS` with no critical or high-severity finding.

The first Ruff checks found import ordering, three formatting changes, and a callable dataclass default. Only mechanical import/layout/default ordering changes were applied; no cap, transition, digest, time, or assertion value changed. Ruff import organization and formatting were run in write mode for those displayed mechanical changes.
- **Final green evidence**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_task_stopping.py tests/unit/domain/test_authority.py tests/contract/test_state_store.py -q` exited 0 at `[100%]`; `uv run --python 3.12 ruff format --check .` exited 0 with `47 files already formatted`; `uv run --python 3.12 ruff check .` exited 0 with `All checks passed!`; `uv run --python 3.12 mypy src` exited 0 with `Success: no issues found in 25 source files`; `uv run --python 3.12 pytest -q` exited 0 at `[100%]` with the two existing platform skips, and collection confirmed `197 tests collected`; `git diff --check` exited 0.

## 2026-08-02 / TASK-009B - Global budget thresholds

- **Base and task**: Task 9B (`TASK-009B`) started from `efd54d59cdb16d94856d88fb027f02ddc1cd1995` on `codex/m1-05-authority`. OpenAI Codex (GPT-5), primary session `/root`, used the `tdd` and `andrej-karpathy-skills:karpathy-guidelines` skills. No subagent or human edited the retained Task 9B files.
- **Threshold authority**: the total `GlobalBudgetMetric` mapping covers all eight SPEC metrics and reads each enforced ceiling from the transactionally revalidated approved Budget. The fixed warning threshold comes only from `V01_MECHANISM_LIMITS.warning_percent`. The journal has no warning-percent or ceiling input, `AtomicAction` carries neither value, and lease issuance passes only the Budget digest so each adapter re-reads `concurrent_worker_ceiling` inside the write transaction. The exact interface regression covers both adapters and fails if any of those three caller-selected cap surfaces returns.
- **Warning and stop behavior**: memory and SQLite persist at most one warning per Run/Budget revision/metric/threshold. At a ceiling, the admitted action or producer mutation commits before `BUDGET_STOP_REQUESTED`; new dispatch closes under the canonical `BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` cause while an admitted model intent or lease remains available to settle. Active-runtime threshold evaluation uses the trusted monotonic reading, persists exact Decimal seconds, leaves the interval open, and preserves cumulative nanoseconds for Task 11B.
- **Producer coverage**: Task 9B covers exactly six production producers available in M1-05: `PLANNING_REQUESTS`, `MODEL_CALLS`, `INPUT_TOKENS`, `OUTPUT_TOKENS`, and `COST_RESERVE_USD` through authorized model reservation/settlement, plus `CONCURRENT_WORKERS` through Workspace Lease issue/expiry. It does not claim eight-producer coverage. The missing concrete producers are `ACTIVE_RUN_SECONDS`, owned by Task 11B, and `TASKS`, owned by Task 12A.
- **Authorized Task 9A test correction**: `test_active_runtime_below_ceiling_does_not_append_audit` was renamed to `test_active_runtime_threshold_appends_exact_warning_audit`. Its only stale premise was Task 9B's legitimate 80-percent crossing behavior; the replacement assertions are stronger and exact: one sequence advance, `GLOBAL_BUDGET_USAGE_SETTLED`, the complete `BudgetWarning` payload, and dispatch still open. No other test in `tests/unit/domain/test_task_stopping.py` changed.
- **Unblock red evidence**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_task_stopping.py::test_active_runtime_below_ceiling_does_not_append_audit -q` exited 1 with `AssertionError: assert 2 == 1`. `uv run --python 3.12 ruff format --check .` exited 1 and named exactly `memory.py`, `sqlite.py`, and `test_global_budget_producers.py`; `uv run --python 3.12 ruff check .` exited 1 with 13 findings. - **Focused green evidence**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_budget_thresholds.py tests/integration/test_global_budget_producers.py tests/unit/domain/test_authority.py tests/unit/domain/test_model_retry.py tests/unit/domain/test_task_stopping.py -q` exited 0 with all 36 displayed cases green. `git diff --check` exited 0.

## 2026-08-02 / TASK-009C - Persist and classify action deadlines

- **Base and task**: Task 9C (`TASK-009C`) started from `75fb5765c7290faf96dfa1cf21cb90794084ca67` on `codex/m1-05-authority`. OpenAI Codex (GPT-5), primary session `/root`, used the `tdd`, `writing-plans`, and `andrej-karpathy-skills:karpathy-guidelines` skills. No subagent, assisting agent, or human edited the retained Task 9C files.
- **Actual first red**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_action_deadlines.py::test_ordinary_and_check_deadlines_use_fixed_v01_limits -q` exited 1 during collection with `ImportError: cannot import name 'ActionDeadline' from 'apexcrew.domain.authority'` and `ERROR: found no collectors`. The plan's predicted missing-`open_action_deadline` `AttributeError` was unreachable because the test imports `ActionDeadline` before its body runs.
- **In-memory red/green**: after the value types and Authority route existed, the same exact selector exited 1 with `AttributeError: 'InMemoryStateStore' object has no attribute 'record_action_deadline'`, then exited 0 with one displayed pass after the copied-map transaction implementation. `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_action_deadlines.py::test_unobservable_ordinary_timeout_is_indeterminate tests/unit/domain/test_action_deadlines.py::test_declared_check_timeout_has_no_passing_receipt -q` exited 0 with two displayed passes.
- **SQLite red/green**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_action_deadlines.py::test_deadline_and_timeout_decision_survive_sqlite_restart -q` first exited 1 with `AttributeError: 'SqliteStateStore' object has no attribute 'record_action_deadline'`. After additive migration 9 alone, the required rerun produced the identical `AttributeError`; the plan's predicted missing-bound-parameter insert was impossible because no method performed an insert. After the methods were added through `_commit_state_and_event`, the first run exposed `StateConflict: CURRENT_BUDGET_BINDING_MISMATCH`; using the transactionally revalidated approved-budget record, which is the same source read by Authority and by the test seeding API, made the selector green. Its final parameterized run exited 0 with two displayed passes covering ordinary and check decisions after reopen.
- **Closed timeout outcomes**: ordinary timeout settlement records `INDETERMINATE`, charges the full reservation classification, and removes the intent from recoverable unsettled work. Declared-check timeout settlement records only `INFRASTRUCTURE_UNCERTAINTY`, leaves the exact check intent and frozen snapshot binding recoverable, and carries neither semantic result nor Receipt. `TimeoutDecision` rejects runtime `PASS`, `FAILED`, or any other non-closed outcome shape. The focused value-object selector first failed with `Failed: DID NOT RAISE <class 'ValueError'>` for `FAILED`, then exited 0 after the closed-outcome validation.
- **Trusted deadline authority**: the plan exposed five caller-controlled authority inputs: `action_class`, `started_at`, current `now`, `check_id`, and `snapshot_digest`. Retained `open_action_deadline` accepts only Run, durable intent, and expected sequence. Authority obtains time from its UTC clock, derives class and check bindings from the canonical durable effect intent, and reads 120/600 seconds only from `V01_MECHANISM_LIMITS`. `deadline_state` and `settle_timeout` read the same trusted clock internally; settlement before expiry raises `ACTION_DEADLINE_NOT_EXPIRED` with zero state or Audit mutation. Both adapters revalidate the intent, current revisions, approved Budget, stored deadline, retry gate, and settle-once condition inside their state-plus-Audit callback.
- **Boundary green**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_action_deadlines.py tests/unit/domain/test_budget_thresholds.py -q` exited 0 at `[100%]` with 23 displayed passing cases. Exact 120/600-second boundaries, early-settlement denial, in-memory and SQLite classification, restart persistence, no passing Receipt, and the Task 9B dispatch gate all pass.
- **Plan defects and interpretations**: Step 10 names undefined `AuthorityError`; the existing closed rejection is `AuthorityDenied`. Step 11 calls undefined `_settle_timeout_successor`, which is not in PLAN's exhaustive permitted undefined-private-helper list; its semantics were implemented explicitly in each adapter. Steps 14-15 prescribe explicit SQLite transactions, but `_commit_state_and_event` owns the transaction and the standing constraint forbids nesting, so both writes use its mutation callback. Step 14's predicted insert failure cannot occur after tables alone. The plan treats uppercase `DECLARED_CHECK` as a caller argument, while the durable Task 15 `ToolIntent.kind` is lowercase `check`; deriving from the durable intent resolves both the naming mismatch and the caller-selectable 600-second ceiling. The plan does not check expiry in `settle_timeout`, so it would allow an early timeout settlement; retained code denies it. The plan's in-memory model had no representation for an `INDETERMINATE` intent, so a separate copied/published set was required to match SQLite's existing effect-intent state.
- **Debt disposition**: no listed debt item is closed by Task 9C. Ten of twelve records one warning and enters `AWAITING_PLAN_APPROVAL`; twelve of twelve persists and journals the Plan first, closes dispatch, enters `PAUSED`, and appends `BUDGET_STOP_REQUESTED` immediately after `PLAN_PROPOSED`. ## 2026-08-02 / TASK-012B - Run the bounded read-only planning loop

- **Base and task**: Task 12B (`TASK-012B`) started from `08bda573114debf1de5728aec67afe76014c76e1` on `codex/m1-07-planning-start`. OpenAI Codex (GPT-5), primary session `/root`, used `writing-plans` and `andrej-karpathy-skills:karpathy-guidelines`. No subagent, assisting agent, worktree change, or human edit was used.
- **Observed red**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_planning_loop.py::test_planning_never_dispatches_before_bootstrap_approval tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_planning_snapshot_reader.py::test_snapshot_reader_rejects_nonregular_tree_entry_before_blob_read -q` exited 4 during collection with `ImportError: cannot import name 'BoundedPlanningReadGateway' from 'apexcrew.domain.coordination'`, `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'apexcrew.adapters.repository.planning'`, and `ERROR: found no collectors` for both node IDs. The plan's prediction that `apexcrew.domain.coordination` was absent was stale because Task 12A had already created that module.
- **Bounded planning behavior**: the Coordinator accepts exactly one closed planning action per RuntimeService boundary. Reads are pinned to the authorized Run/repository/base/scope snapshot, use structured Git operations, reject nonregular and noncanonical entries before blob content, enforce manifest/file/search/total-return caps, settle canonical returned bytes durably, and retain no newly exposed content on overflow. Additive SQLite migration 14 and copied-map memory state remain behaviorally matched. Recovered reads, failures, invalid actions, and Plan submissions settle the exact journaled marker under the exact consumed planning Permit in the same state-plus-Audit transaction and do not redispatch the model.
- **Authority and barrier closure**: `AuthorityModelClient` opens a deterministic runtime barrier before every reservation, refreshes sequence and counters, reserves every first attempt and known-closed retry through `Authority.reserve_model_attempt`, uses only `V01_MECHANISM_LIMITS.ordinary_action_timeout_seconds`, and settles the barrier after the attempt. A denial makes zero provider calls. Retry coverage observes `IN_FLIGHT` at both provider calls and proves two provider attempts consume one logical planning request. Debt #6(a), retry budget revalidation, and #6(b), an open barrier before every reservation, are closed by production code plus tests.
- **Caller-trust corrections**: the plan's caller-derived two-minute retry window was rejected; the fixed mechanism deadline is read from `V01_MECHANISM_LIMITS`. Repository identity, pinned base, scope, snapshot, returned bytes, planning count, recovered marker, Permit, and revision bindings are revalidated by the mechanism. Retry attempts do not increment `PLANNING_REQUESTS` or trip the logical-turn ceiling reached by their own first attempt. Recovery required a separate `current_for_recovery` authorization view because the plan's sole `current(run_id)` view observes the already-incremented count and cannot process an eighth committed turn.
- **Subprocess debt**: Task 7B's production `capture_output=True` path was replaced with concurrent bounded stdout/stderr drains, a fixed 30-second deadline, fail-closed overflow, and an already-exited-process kill guard. The 2,000,001-byte test is cross-platform. This closes the implementation and test substance of debt #10 for Git capture, but `AI4SE-ops/DEBT.md` is outside the exact stage and remains unchanged.
- **Focused green evidence**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_planning_loop.py tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_planning_snapshot_reader.py tests/integration/test_planning_authorization.py -q` exited 0 with 17 displayed passes. `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_planning_loop.py tests/unit/adapters/repository/test_planning_snapshot_reader.py tests/integration/test_planning_authorization.py tests/contract/test_state_store.py -q` exited 0 at `[100%]` with 70 displayed passes before the final three safety regressions were added; all three are included in the final full-suite gate.
- **Plan defects and interpretations**: the red prediction was stale; the source snippets omit the runtime barrier required by SPEC line 299; the retry snippet reuses a caller-selected attempt window; `credential_profile=None` is described as valid for `ScriptedMockLLM` even though the existing Authority correctly returns `CREDENTIAL_UNAVAILABLE`; the read-gateway snippet catches an adapter exception from the domain layer; the subprocess snippet uses unbounded `capture_output=True`; and the recovery prose requires marker settlement for Plan submission while the initial split implementation path had no recovery-aware graph transaction. The plan also failed to distinguish logical planning requests from provider retries, making a retry consume another planning turn and potentially deny itself. The retained code resolves each issue without weakening an existing denial.

Requested-model-ID persistence/validation, injectable durable IDs and request-sensitive `ScriptedMockLLM`, standalone dependency-order validation, secret-policy default composition/HMAC/non-disclosure coverage, and recursive reservation tamper inspection remain outside Task 12B and were not deepened.

- **Pre-task record**: fresh implementation subagent OpenAI Codex (GPT-5), session `/root/r3_01_fix001_legacy_repair_impl`, starts from base `1a92d8eefb9a0bb0180f165f5efc4a2ca369c732` on `codex/m1-r3-model-provenance`.
- **Quality-review P1**: a newly completed SQLite turn could be reclassified as legacy by deleting both the `response_requested_model_id` SQL value and the serialized dispatch key, allowing restart recovery to release its action.
- **Exact red command**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_model_requests.py tests/contract/test_state_store.py tests/integration/test_model_restart.py -q`.
- **Allowed path set**: `src/apexcrew/domain/model.py`, `src/apexcrew/adapters/state/memory.py`, `src/apexcrew/adapters/state/sqlite.py`, `tests/unit/domain/test_model_requests.py`, `tests/contract/test_state_store.py`, `tests/integration/test_model_restart.py`, and `AGENT_LOG.md` only. The public seam is `DurableModelClient` restart recovery; direct SQLite manipulation is limited to constructing a physical schema-v15 migration input.
- **Observed red**: the exact selector exited `1` with `67 passed, 1 failed`. `test_new_committed_completion_cannot_be_reclassified_as_legacy` deleted both the current completion's SQLite response-requested ID and serialized dispatch key; `DurableModelClient.recover_committed()` returned `COMPLETED` rather than raising the expected closed storage-binding conflict.
- **Implementation**: migration 16 now adds constrained `response_requested_model_binding` columns to `model_attempts` and `model_turns`, defaulting pre-migration rows to `LEGACY`. Every new attempt settlement writes `BOUND`; every new committed completion writes `BOUND`. Reads accept `LEGACY` only when both the SQL value is null and the dispatch key is absent. `BOUND` requires an existing dispatch key whose value matches the SQL column, and a committed bound completion additionally requires a non-null response-requested ID. No value is inferred from request JSON.
- **Behavior evidence**: the legacy test physically removes both migration-16 response-ID and binding columns, removes migration `16` from the schema ledger, and removes both serialized keys before reopening through `SqliteStateStore`; the resulting migrated legacy completion recovers through `DurableModelClient`. The current-row tamper test deletes both representations on attempts and the committed turn; `model_attempts()` and public restart recovery each reject, and the empty scripted model remains uncalled.
- **Green evidence**: `uv run --python 3.12 pytest tests/unit/domain/test_model_requests.py tests/contract/test_state_store.py tests/integration/test_model_restart.py -q` exited `0` with `68 passed`; `uv run --python 3.12 mypy src` exited `0` with `Success: no issues found in 33 source files`; `uv run --python 3.12 ruff format --check .` exited `0` with `69 files already formatted`; `uv run --python 3.12 ruff check .` exited `0` with `All checks passed!`; and both cached and working-tree `git diff --check` exited `0` with no output.
